                   This supplement supplies proofs and implementation details for the R48
6                                                                                                  6
                 revision, retaining the R44–R47 results: centered economic transport, direct
7                                                                                                  7
                 full-policy certification, retained-policy recertification, and coupled policy-
8                cost comparisons. It reports all new comparison intervals and separates           8

9                mathematical, numerical, and statistical guarantees. The constructive neu-        9

10               ral backend has its own complete primitive-budget proof below. Complete           10
                 earlier proofs and economic applications are materialized unchanged in
11                                                                                                 11
                 the current source tree.

                                1. NOTATION AND ECONOMIC DOMAINS
10                                                                                                         10

11
         Dates are 0, . . . , T; B0 = 1 and Bt = ∏ j<t β j with β j > 0. The state domain is               11

12
     invariant under all admissible actions and the declared innovation law. The fixed- 12
13
     policy operator is Ttπ v( x ) = ct ( x, πt ( x )) + β t ρt (v( Ft ( x, πt ( x ), Z ))). The Bellman   13

14
     operator is its infimum over feasible current actions. Every cash-invariance and                      14

15
     monotonicity statement is restricted to a common domain containing the values,                        15

16
     fitted functions, and constant translates used in the comparison. This requirement                    16

17
     is substantive for recursive utility.                                                                 17

18
         For compact continuous expected-cost problems with continuous feasible corre- 18
19
     spondences, continuous integrals, and measurable minimizers, backward induction                       19

20
     produces the Bellman optimum. An adapted policy cannot lower its conditional                          20

21
     continuation cost below the Bellman value at any date. Induction therefore proves                     21

22
     optimality against history-dependent admissible controls, rather than only Markov                     22

23
     grid policies. The same reasoning applies to the stipulated monotone recursive                        23

24
     evaluation when its conditional recursion and measurable selection conditions hold. 24
25
     The Gaussian entropic construction in the original article uses separate moment                       25

26
     margins rather than compactness.                                                                      26

15                                                                                                15

16                                                                                                16

17                                                                                                17

                       T −1                               T −1

29   We prove Vt∗ ≥ f t − at and Jtπ ≤ f t + bt simultaneously. The terminal claim is the         29

30   terminal enclosure. For the lower induction,                                                 30

1    For the upper induction,                                                                    1

2                                                                                                2

3
                Jtπ = Ttπ Jtπ+1 ≤ Ttπ ( f t+1 + bt+1 ) ≤ f t − ℓt + ηt + β t bt+1 = f t + bt .   3

4                                                                                                4
     Subtraction yields the theorem. Additive shifts dt change each residual by dt −
5                                                                                                5
     β t dt+1 and the terminal residual by d T ; widths and action gaps are unchanged. This
6                                                                                                6
     proves the invariance without assuming a contraction factor less than one.
7                                                                                                7
         For retained-policy recertification, new residual enclosures are substituted in
8                                                                                                8
     exactly the same induction. The old actor allowance remains valid because its
9                                                                                                9
     hypothesis is a uniform statement about the original actor and the unchanged
10                                                                                               10
     functions. It has no dependence on the mesh on which the new residual happens to
11                                                                                               11
     be validated. The terminal allowance can also be retained. Intersecting two valid
12                                                                                               12
     residual intervals preserves validity and cannot increase width. In the executed
13                                                                                               13
     diagnostics the new residuals are used directly, with the old actor and terminal
14                                                                                               14
     enclosures; the record contains every component needed to reconstruct the bound.
15                                                                                               15
         The zero-shift case does not imply that constant residuals disappear from all
16                                                                                               16
     value brackets. They disappear from the policy-loss bound through upper-minus-
17                                                                                               17
     lower cancellation. Keeping signed endpoints is consequently important for direct
18                                                                                               18
     cost scores as well as for sharp policy bounds.

                    4. VERIFICATION GEOMETRY AND NUMERICAL ENCLOSURES
21                                                                                               21
                                   4.1. The scalar primitive economy
22                                                                                               22

23       The scalar state is x ∈ [0, 1], action a ∈ [0, 1/4], horizon four, and discount 15/16. 23
24   The transition is                                                                           24

25                                                                                               25

26
                    F ( x, a, z) = clip[0,1] {(13/16) x + a + (1/16) x (1 − x ) + z},            26

27                                                                                               27
     with z ∈ {−1/16, 0, 1/16} and probabilities (1/4, 1/2, 1/4). The cost is
28                                                                                               28

29
                         c( x, a) = ( x − 11/16)2 + pa2 + 4a4 + 2(3/8 − x )2+ ,                  29

30                                                                                               30

31   and terminal cost is g( x ) = 2( x − 11/16)2 + 2(3/8 − x )2+ . The two evaluations are 31
32   expectation and the entropic certainty equivalent ρθ (v) = θ −1 log Eeθv with θ = 1; 32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                          5

1    expectation is θ = 0. The discrete law here is an economic primitive, unlike the           1

2    continuous uniform law of the coupled experiment.                                          2

3      For a scalar ReLU network, exact dyadic coefficients permit rational breakpoints         3

4    and slopes. The largest absolute slope bounds its Lipschitz constant Lt . The Bellman      4

5    state and action constants used by the verifier are                                        5

6                                                                                               6
           Ltx = 2.875 + (15/16)(0.875) Lt+1 ,           Lta = p/2 + 1/4 + (15/16) Lt+1 .
7                                                                                               7

8    For N + 1 state nodes and A + 1 action nodes, the action cover allowance is Lta /(8A)      8

9    and the residual state allowance is ( Lt + Ltx )/(2N ). Each residual endpoint includes    9

10   the state allowance; the nodal Bellman lower bound includes the action cover. The          10

11   nearest-node actor allowance adds the selected nodal action gap, action cover, and         11

12   Ltx /N. All operations are outward enclosed.                                               12

13     The terminal residual is piecewise quadratic. Its extrema occur at exact network         13

14   breakpoints, the cost kink, endpoints, or stationary points in each piece. The imple- 14
15   mentation enumerates these candidates using rational arithmetic and rounds the             15

16   extrema outward. It therefore does not replace an exact terminal calculation with          16

17   an uncharged sampled check.                                                                17

28   Indeed, strong convexity gives a quadratic lower model; adding ν(1′ b − h) gives a         28

29   lower bound on the constrained infimum, and minimizing the separable quadratic             29

31   Interval lower evaluation and the feasible proposal’s upper value enclose the              31

32   continuous-action infimum without trusting numerical first-order convergence.              32

11                                                                                              11

12                                                                                              12
     the original records.
13                                                                                              13
         If the gradient of the fitted current continuation has Lipschitz constant Mt and the
14                                                                                              14

15                                                                                              15
     cells of side 1/N has residual error at most ( Mt + Ht+1 )/(4N 2 ). To see this, condi-
16                                                                                              16

17                                                                                              17

18                                                                                              18
     gives Lh2 /4. The source-bound derivative envelopes, rather than empirical finite
19                                                                                              19
     differences, supply these constants. The terminal allowance uses ( MT + 18)/(4N 2 ).
20                                                                                              20
         In the retained-policy diagnostic, these residual terms are refined at N = 256, but
21                                                                                              21
     the actor term remains the one certified for N = 128. The record may also contain
22                                                                                              22
     the fine-grid proposed actor’s allowance as part of the inherited verifier output.
23                                                                                              23
     That allowance is not used to claim an unchanged-policy result.

25                                                                                              25
                    4.3. Arithmetic assumptions and implementation boundary
26                                                                                              26

27       The inherited interval implementation applies outward rounding to elementary           27

28   binary operations and encloses its exponential and logarithm approximations by             28

29   explicit series remainders. Stored network and actor coefficients are interpreted as       29

30   exact dyadics. Analytic innovation moments are enclosed using interval arithmetic,         30

31   including cancellation. Exact rational breakpoint calculations and independently           31

32   checked square roots are used where specified.                                             32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                                    7

1      The resulting certificates concern the defined policies, with numerical enclosures                                 1

2    around their evaluation. The original rounded-state and execution accounts remain                                    2

3    separate. The new retained-policy bound does not silently assert that an arbitrary                                   3

4    hardware implementation with different state acquisition, optimizer, or clipping                                     4

5    conventions inherits the same target. A deployed implementation must satisfy the                                     5

6    execution assumptions or add its own allowance.                                                                      6

7                                                                                                                         7
                 5. PAIRED POLICY COSTS: PROBABILITY AND NUMERICAL ERROR
8                                                                                                                         8

9                       5.1. An unbiased score with certificate-derived range                                             9

10                                                                                                                        10
       Under expectation and a fixed policy, the cost score is
11                                                                                                                        11

12      Zm = f 0m ( x0 ) +   ∑      Bt ( Qt ( f tm+1 ; Xtm , πtm ) − f tm ( Xtm )) + BT ( g( XTm ) − f Tm ( XTm )).       12
                             t< T
13                                                                                                                        13

14   Taking expectations cancels the fitted values at every date, including both endpoints. 14
15   The score has expected value equal to actual policy cost even if the networks are                                    15

16   poor approximations. To obtain bounded support, write the centered summand                                           16

17   as the nonnegative selected-action gap minus the signed Bellman residual. It lies                                    17

18   in [−ut , ηt − ℓt ]. The terminal term lies in [−u T , −ℓ T ]. Thus the score interval has                           18

19   width precisely the corresponding ideal policy bound. Subtracting two scores has a                                   19

20   support width equal to the sum of their policy bounds, but its expectation is the                                    20

21   direct difference of policy costs. The distinction is that the scores are evaluated along                            21

22   the policies’ trajectories; two regret upper bounds are never used as a surrogate                                    22

23   observation of the cost difference.                                                                                  23

24                                                                                                                        24

25                       5.2. Continuous innovations through uniform bins                                                 25

26                                                                                                                        26
       For each comparison, draw independent integer arrays Kitj uniformly in
27
     {0, . . . , 240 − 1}. A bin represents the continuous interval                                                       27

28                                                                                                                        28
                                             2Kitj                   2(Kitj + 1)
                                                                                    
                                    1                        1
29
                                               40
                                                     −1 ,                          −1        .                            29

30
                                    32       2              32          240                                               30

31   The exact uniform innovation law can be represented by a uniform bin followed                                        31

32   by a uniform draw inside it. The implementation encloses every draw in that bin                                      32
     8

1    and needs no additional within-bin random number. Both policies use the same                1

2    innovation bins but propagate their own states and choose their own actions.                2

3        At each date and in each coordinate, the actor is the half-up nearest-node rule. If     3

4    a state interval meets more than one cell, the implementation includes all eligible         4

5    node actions and propagates their componentwise hull. Such a hull need not itself           5

6    be a feasible action, but interval evaluation over the hull still encloses every feasible   6

7    action actually selected; it is used for enclosure, not as a new policy. The bounding       7

8    arithmetic covers network values, analytic expectations, transition, stage cost, and        8

9    terminal cost. Intersecting sample score enclosures with the deterministic support          9

10   is valid for every bin.                                                                     10

11       For a bin Ki , the computed endpoints Ai , Di are deterministic functions with Ai ≤     11

12   Zi ≤ Di for every within-bin realization. Hence EAi ≤ EZi ≤ EDi . Independence of           12

13   bin draws makes each endpoint sequence independent and identically distributed,             13

14   conditional on the frozen policies. Endpoint widening changes numerical precision,          14

15   not the underlying innovation law.                                                          15

16                                                                                               16

17                                                                                               17

18                                                                                               18
                                    5.3. Simultaneous coverage
19                                                                                               19

20       Let q = 24, α = 0.01, and n = 65536. Scale each endpoint sequence to its deter- 20
21   ministic support of width B. Apply the one-sided empirical Bernstein inequality of          21

22   Maurer and Pontil (2009), Theorem 4, to the lower sequence with reversed sign and           22

23   to the upper sequence in its usual direction, each at tail probability α/(2q). Rescal- 23
24   ing gives the radius in the main article with logarithm log(4q/α) = log(9600). The          24

25   union bound over all 48 tails yields simultaneous coverage at least 0.99. Dependence        25

26   between comparisons is not excluded or required to vanish; only within-comparison           26

27   trajectory independence enters this argument.                                               27

28       The code computes means by outward pairwise summation. If [m, m] contains the           28

29   true sample mean, interval squares of xi − [m, m] bound every squared deviation             29

30   from that mean. Their outward sum divided by n − 1 therefore bounds sample                  30

31   variance above. The logarithm uses the inherited interval series. A proposed floating       31

32   square root is checked by exact rational squaring and enlarged until it is an upper         32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           9

1    bound. Since the radius is increasing in variance, range, and logarithm, these           1

2    substitutions preserve coverage.                                                         2

3      This is a probability theorem for the stipulated independent simulation model. 3
4    The fixed PCG64 seed identifies the reproducible realization; it is not a mathematical   4

5    proof that a deterministic sequence is independent. The comparison states, policies,     5

6    sample count, and multiplicity are fixed before the new simulations. No optional         6

7    stopping or policy selection is performed. All 24 intervals contain zero, so no sign     7

8    or equivalence conclusion is made.                                                       8

22     Operations count Bellman transitions for scalar services and neuron expectations    22

23   for the coupled service; these are different units, not cross-method speed estimates. 23
24   The additional verification clocks include computations of fine-grid action propos- 24
25   als even though those proposals are not deployed. They do not repeat training         25

26   or reconstruct the original service clock. Jobs were scheduled concurrently on a      26

27   resource-limited local host; these clocks describe those diagnostics and cannot be    27

28   read as isolated cross-method speed measurements.                                     28

29     The original records expose state coverage, action coverage, nodal gaps, and        29

30   terminal bounds. A representation/optimizer decomposition is not identifiable         30

31   from them: different parameter errors and fitting trajectories can produce the same   31

32   signed Bellman residual. We report the available components as an error account,      32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           11

1    not a causal identification of each source of approximation error. Training residuals     1

2    are available as separate diagnostics and are not added twice to a certificate that       2

3    already evaluates the trained functions.                                                  3

4      A fresh reproduction obtains the two pinned input artifacts, verifies their archive     4

5    digests, and uses a clean result directory. The study commands deliberately refuse        5

6    to overwrite completed records. The deterministic audit reconstructs target counts        6

7    and signed comparisons from all original records, checks retained network and             7

8    actor hashes, and reconstructs every new confidence endpoint from stored moments          8

9    and support. Regression tests exercise interval arithmetic, exact finite-state policy     9

10   inequalities, offset invariance, and source identities. The release manifest binds        10

11   every active source, table, result, and compiled document.                                11

12                                                                                             12
                          7. RELATIONSHIP TO THE RETAINED THEORY
13                                                                                             13

14     The full controlled-diffusion and viscosity analysis, economic boundary and             14

15   transversality conditions, recursive utility and endogenous-preference models,            15

16   temporal selves, games, quadratic hidden-factor construction, numerical acceptance        16

17   theorems, and historical experiments remain in their unchanged source files and           17

18   compiled companions. The new main article does not purport to re-prove them               18

19   by citing the nonlinear experiment. The preservation map records file hashes and          19

20   LaTeX labels, and the response gives the location of each current answer to the           20

21   referee.                                                                                  21

22     The current revision supplies an integrated paper and new proofs and evidence. 22
23   The new constructive catalogue supplies a separate horizon/price study and re- 23
24   peated isolated timings for its declared backend. An adaptive-grid comparator, a          24

25   nonlinear dimension frontier, and repeated isolated comparisons for the original          25

26   optimizers remain distinct evidence requirements; retained-policy and paired-cost         26

27   results are not substitutes for them.                                                     27

28                                                                                             28
                 8. CONSTRUCTIVE NEURAL POLICIES FROM PRIMITIVE MODULI
29                                                                                             29

30     This section supplies the complete implementation-independent proof of the con- 30
31   structive theorem in the main article. Its assumptions are a compact state set X ⊂ Rd ,   31

32   a common compact action set A ⊂ Ra , a finite horizon, nonnegative discount factors,      32
     12

1    an invariant transition, a common innovation law, and state/action-independent                           1

2    monotone cash-invariant conditional evaluation on a common bounded-function                              2

3    domain. The primitive moduli are those in the main article’s constructive section. 3
4    Effective compactness and finite certified numerical evaluation are used only for                        4

5    finite termination, not for the analytic inequalities.                                                   5

6                                                                                                             6

7                                    8.1. Bellman moduli and labels                                           7

8      If ∥u − v∥∞ ≤ r, then v − r ≤ u ≤ v + r. Monotonicity and cash invariance give                         8

9    |ρt (u) − ρt (v)| ≤ r. Consequently, when f t+1 is Lt+1 -Lipschitz,                                      9

10                                                                                                            10

11
                   | Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) − Qt ( f t+1 ; y, b)| ≤ Lt ∥ x − y∥1 + Lta ∥ a − b∥1 ,                11

12                                                                                                            12
     where Lt = Cx,t + β t Fx,t Lt+1 and Lta = Ca,t + β t Fa,t Lt+1 . Infima over the same action
13
     set preserve the state modulus. Compactness and continuity give minima. Alter- 13
14                                                                                                            14
     natively, the same argument uses arbitrarily close minimizers and then takes their
15                                                                                                            15
     error to zero.
16                                                                                                            16
       Let Ht = Tt f t+1 , qij = Qt ( f t+1 ; xi , a j ), and |qbij − qij | ≤ e. The state and action grids
17                                                                                                            17
     have radii h, k. Since every grid action is feasible,
18                                                                                                            18

19                             yi = min qbij ≥ min qij − e ≥ Ht ( xi ) − e.                                   19
                                        j           j
20                                                                                                            20
     Choose an action minimizer a∗ and a grid action within k of it. Then
21                                                                                                            21

22
                                    yi ≤ qij∗ + e ≤ Ht ( xi ) + L a k + e.                                    22

23                                                                                                            23

24   These are bounds on the label array actually produced by the own-future recursion. 24
25   They do not use Vt∗+1 and do not assume that a regression loss is small.                                 25

26                                                                                                            26

27
                              8.2. Envelope, residual, and selected action                                    27

28     For arbitrary finite labels yi , define Ey ( x ) = mini {yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 }. Each cone is             28

29   L-Lipschitz. For a minimizing cone at x ′ , Ey ( x ) − Ey ( x ′ ) ≤ L∥ x − x ′ ∥1 , and reversing        29

30   the points proves the Lipschitz assertion. If maxi |yi − yi′ | ≤ δ, then every cone shifts               30

31   by at most δ, so ∥ Ey − Ey′ ∥∞ ≤ δ. In particular, numerical label errors cannot inflate                 31

32   the certified slope.                                                                                     32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                13

1      For every node, yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 ≥ Ht ( xi ) − e + L∥ x − xi ∥1 ≥ Ht ( x ) − e. Taking       1

2    the minimum proves the lower residual bound. Choosing a nearest node i ( x ) gives              2

3                                                                                                    3
                                            a                             a
4
                  Ey ( x ) ≤ Ht ( xi( x) ) + L k + e + Lh ≤ Ht ( x ) + L k + e + 2Lh.                4

5                                                                                                    5
     If j(i ) minimizes the numerical queries, then
6                                                                                                    6

7                        qi,j(i) ≤ qbi,j(i) + e = yi + e ≤ Ht ( xi ) + L a k + 2e.                   7

8                                                                                                    8

9    The difference of the chosen-action value and Ht has state modulus at most 2L. 9
10   Thus the nearest-node actor has gap at most L a k + 2e + 2Lh. A deterministic rule for          10

11   ties among finitely many closed Voronoi cells gives a Borel actor. Any tied nearest             11

12   node satisfies the same inequality. Because the action set is common, this actor is             12

13   feasible at every state. This proof would not establish feasibility for an unrestricted         13

14   state-dependent correspondence.                                                                 14

15     At the terminal date, HT = g and there is no action minimization. Labels within               15

16   eT of g yield −eT ≤ f T − g ≤ eT + 2L g h T . Starting from the terminal network and            16

17   proceeding backward establishes the assumed continuation moduli at every date. 17
18   The policy theorem, proved earlier in this supplement, then yields                              18

19                                                                                                   19
               J0π ( x ) − V0 ( x ) ≤ ∑ Bt (2Lta k t + 4et + 4Lt ht ) + BT (2eT + 2L g h T ).
20                                                                                                   20
                                   t< T
21                                                                                                   21
     The comparison is with the optimum over all admissible adapted policies, not just
22                                                                                                   22
     policies constant on the grid. The action covering allowance is precisely what makes
23                                                                                                   23
     the continuous-action comparison possible.
24                                                                                                   24

25                                                                                                   25
                               8.3. Realization and finite termination
26                                                                                                   26

27     An absolute value uses two ReLU gates. A sum of d absolute values with affine co- 27
28   efficients computes one cone. The identity min(u, v) = u − max(u − v, 0) computes               28

29   pairwise minima. A balanced finite tree consumes K cones in O(log K ) stages, with              29

30   O(K ) additional gates. Positive and negative channels represent signed intermedi- 30
31   ate scalars in an ordinary feed-forward realization. The total number of coefficients           31

32   is O(dK ); a bounded-fan-in realization can replace each affine sum by a logarithmic- 32
     14

1    depth addition tree. This is an explicit network with weights determined by labels,            1

2    state nodes, and the primitive upper modulus.                                                  2

3      All constants may be replaced by computable rational upper bounds. The labels                3

4    can be rational rounded midpoints of certified numerical intervals. State/action               4

5    covers must be supplied with verifiable covering and feasibility radii, rather than            5

6    presumed because a set is topologically compact. For the budget s = ε/ ∑ Bt , the              6

7    nonterminal terms satisfy                                                                      7

8                                                                                                   8

9                                 2(s/8) + 4(s/8) + 4(s/16) = s,                                    9

10                                                                                                  10
     and terminal terms satisfy 2(s/4) + 2(s/4) = s. Positive-radius covers have finitely
11                                                                                                  11
     many nodes and numerical queries reach the specified tolerances by hypothesis.
12                                                                                                  12
     Backward construction therefore takes finite work and the policy loss is at most ε.
13                                                                                                  13
     This is a deterministic algorithm theorem. It does not turn a finite implementation
14                                                                                                  14
     cap into a termination guarantee, and it does not provide a convergence theorem
15                                                                                                  15
     for Adam, stochastic gradient descent, or an arbitrary nonconvex loss.
16                                                                                                  16
       The work expression in the article counts all state–action–innovation evaluations
17                                                                                                  17
     of the already constructed future network. If numerical precision increases with
18                                                                                                  18
     the requested tolerance, the bit cost of each operation and of each weight must
19                                                                                                  19
     also increase in the account. A tensor cover retains its exponential state/action
20                                                                                                  20
     dimension factors. Analytic or sparse integration can reduce the innovation work
21                                                                                                  21
     but does not remove the covering factors merely by being called neural computation.
22                                                                                                  22

23                                                                                                  23

24                                  8.4. Exact scalar compilation                                   24

25     Let 0 = x0 < · · · < x N = 1 and let the labels be arbitrary rational numbers. Define        25

26                                                                                                  26

27                                   zi = min{y j + L| xi − x j |}.                                 27
                                            j
28                                                                                                  28

29   A left-to-right pass computes the minimum over j ≤ i and a right-to-left pass                  29

30   incorporates j ≥ i. The result is the greatest L-Lipschitz vector lying below the              30

31   labels: any such vector w satisfies wi ≤ w j + L| xi − x j | ≤ y j + L| xi − x j | for every j. 31
32   In particular |zi+1 − zi | ≤ L( xi+1 − xi ).                                                   32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                   15

1      For x ∈ [ xi , xi+1 ], every cone centered to the left is represented by the best left          1

2    intercept and every cone centered to the right by the best right intercept. Therefore             2

3                                                                                                      3
                       Ey ( x ) = min{zi + L( x − xi ), zi+1 + L( xi+1 − x )}.
4                                                                                                      4

5                                                                                                      5
     For L > 0, the intersection is
6                                                                                                      6
                                  x + x i +1 z i +1 − z i
7                             ri = i        +             ∈ [ x i , x i +1 ] .                         7
                                     2            2L
8                                                                                                      8
     When the intersection is interior it is the only additional knot; endpoint intersections
9                                                                                                      9
     cause no duplicate knot. When L = 0 the envelope is constant. This proves equality
10                                                                                                     10
     of the compiled piecewise-linear function and the defining network everywhere,
11                                                                                                     11
     including non-dyadic intersections and ties.
12                                                                                                     12
       For rational knots r j , values v j , and slopes s j , the antiderivative on one cell is
13                                                                                                     13

14                          A( x ) = A(r j ) + v j ( x − r j ) + 21 s j ( x − r j )2 .                 14

15                                                                                                     15

16
     Rational trapezoidal accumulation computes A(r j ) exactly. Under a continuous                    16

17
     uniform innovation on [−σ, σ], the own-future expectation is [ A(b + σ) − A(b −                   17

18
     σ )]/(2σ). This is an identity under the original law, not a finite-bin change of the             18

19
     economy.                                                                                          19

20
       The numerical evaluator encloses each rational coefficient and each native arith- 20
21
     metic operation outward. At a query near a rounded non-dyadic knot, it resolves the               21

22
     cell using the exact rational knot and the exact rational value of the binary64 query. 22
23
     It does not use a rounded knot as a silent change of the partition. An uncertain                  23

24
     antiderivative argument is evaluated at its midpoint and expanded by sup | f | times              24

25
     its radius; an uncertain innovation center is expanded by Lip( f ) times its radius. 25
26
     Intersections with [0, 1] discard only rounding overhang when the exact primitive                 26

27
     argument is known to lie in that invariant interval. These steps separate analytic                27

28
     integration from arithmetic rounding.                                                             28

29                                                                                                     29
                       8.5. Matched spline account and measurement scope
30                                                                                                     30

31     For the spline comparator, linear interpolation of labels with node errors between              31

32   −e and L a k + e gives a residual interval [−e − Lh, L a k + e + Lh]. Indeed the inter- 32
     16

1    polating weights are nonnegative and sum to one, and their weighted distance           1

2    to the query point is at most h on the uniform one-dimensional mesh. Its width         2

3    is L a k + 2e + 2Lh, the same form as the envelope width. The nearest-node actor       3

4    allowance is also the same form. The spline’s actual exact slope, not the neural       4

5    primitive upper bound, is propagated into its next date’s query modulus. Both          5

6    terminal certificates conservatively use the primitive terminal modulus. Neither       6

7    comparator borrows the other’s targets.                                                7

8      The catalogue records exact rational gap formulas, upper binary64 endpoints,         8

9    all interval-error allowances, defining labels, compiled nodes, and selected actions   9

10   for every rung. It also records evaluated Bellman queries, continuous-law inte- 10
11   grals, antiderivative queries, binary-search upper counts, deployed actor scalars,     11

12   rational storage quantities, maximum stored numerator/denominator bit lengths,         12

13   checkpoint bytes, process peak resident memory, internal clocks, and process           13

14   clocks. Primitive-object counts are not described as a complete floating-point or      14

15   bit-operation count. Peak memory includes the interpreter and warm-up. The inter- 15
16   nal target clock includes failed rungs and durable checkpoint output; independent      16

17   economic comparison is absent rather than costless. These scope qualifications are     17

18   part of the empirical estimand.                                                        18

19     A first-rung timing repetition is not a new random economic environment. For         19

20   each fixed method/horizon/price combination, the three exact certificate arrays        20

21   and deployed objects should coincide; their clocks need not. Equality of object        21

22   hashes is audited separately from timing dispersion. This supplies reproducibility     22

23   for the declared deterministic generator without inferring a probability of success    23

24   for another training algorithm.                                                        24

                       10. WITNESS-PRESERVING POLICY CONSTRUCTION
30                                                                                         30

31     This section establishes the additional policy sandwich and the witness- 31
32   preserving implementation. It uses the same primitive economy, admissible-policy      32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             19

1    comparison and common evaluation domain as the corresponding main-text                       1

2    results. All inequalities concern the continuation actually constructed from its own         2

3    fitted future.                                                                               3

4                                                                                                 4

5                          10.1. Order comparison and signed accounts                             5

6      Suppose f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ ut and Ttπ f t+1 − f t ≤ dt . Define                              6

7                                                                                                 7
                                            j −1
                                                !                 !
                                     T −1                        T −1
8                             Ut = ∑        ∏ βk          uj +   ∏ βk   uT ,                      8
                                     j=t    k=t                  k=t
9                                                                                                 9

10   with empty products equal to one. The terminal inequality gives VT∗ = g ≥ f T − UT . 10
11   If Vt∗+1 ≥ f t+1 − Ut+1 , monotonicity and cash invariance yield                     11

12                                                                                                12
                 Vt∗ = Tt Vt∗+1 ≥ Tt f t+1 − β t Ut+1 ≥ f t − ut − β t Ut+1 = f t − Ut .
13                                                                                                13

14   The same induction for the fixed-policy map, beginning at g ≤ f T − ℓ T , gives              14

15                                                                                                15
                                     J0π ≤ f 0 + ∑ Bt dt − BT ℓ T .
16                                                                                                16
                                                   t< T
17                                                                                                17
     Subtracting proves the one-sided sandwich. Neither ut nor dt is replaced by an
18                                                                                                18
     absolute value; their signs cancel the effect of arbitrary date-specific changes of
19                                                                                                19
     origin. The lower loss bound follows from Bellman optimality against all admissible
20                                                                                                20
     adapted policies. This argument uses no independence of simulations and no
21                                                                                                21
     linearity of the recursive functional. It is distinct from the expectation-specific
22                                                                                                22
     paired telescoping theorem retained earlier in this supplement.
23                                                                                                23
       The old centered certificate is the special bound dt ≤ ηt − ℓt . Its possible slack is
24                                                                                                24
     due to taking two separate extrema of quantities that share the same fitted value.
25                                                                                                25
     The new witness result controls their sum directly.
26                                                                                                26

27                     10.2. Action transport through the minimum circuit                         27

28                                                                                                28
       At a node xi , the numerical action query attaining yi corresponds to an actual fea-
29                                                                                                29
     sible action ai . The query enclosure gives Q( xi , ai ) ≤ yi + e. Uniform state Lipschitz
30                                                                                                30
     continuity of Q(·, a) therefore implies
31                                                                                                31

32                        Q( x, ai ) − f ( x ) ≤ e + yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 − f ( x )                  32
     20

1    for every node and every state. Selecting an index attaining the minimum cone                    1

2    makes the last three terms sum to zero. Finite minima attain their values, and a                 2

3    fixed ordering of the finite indices defines a Borel selector. The common action set             3

4    is essential: the proof does not transport an action into a state at which that action is        4

5    infeasible.                                                                                      5

6      Combining dt = et with ut = Lta k t + et + 2Lt ht proves the displayed witness gap. 6
7    It also implies the separated actor allowance                                                    7

8                                                                                                     8
                   Qt ( f t+1 ; x, πtw ( x )) − Tt f t+1 ( x ) ≤ et + ut = Lta k t + 2et + 2Lt ht .
9                                                                                                     9

10                                                                                                    10
     Thus the earlier numerical separated budget is also a valid, though weaker, bound
11                                                                                                    11
     for the new witness actor. Using it as an alternative does not substitute the old
12                                                                                                    12
     nearest-node policy. At each nonterminal date, the new sufficient resource choices
13                                                                                                    13
     obey s/4 + 2(s/4) + 2(s/8) = s. The terminal bracket is also at most s. Effective
14                                                                                                    14
     finite covers and certified numerical evaluation then give finite termination.
15                                                                                                    15
       Only the critic is an affine–ReLU circuit. Returning a comparison index and an
16
     attached action is a finite measurable algorithm with a possibly discontinuous policy. 16
17                                                                                                    17
     Classical Lipschitz extension and elementary minimum gates remain credited in
18                                                                                                    18
     the main article. The result does not assert their novelty or claim that an equivalent
19                                                                                                    19
     conventional envelope implementation is impossible.
20                                                                                                    20

21                        10.3. Approximate indices and numerical deployment                          21

22                                                                                                    22
       Let x ′ be an acquired state with ∥ x − x ′ ∥1 ≤ r. Suppose the selected index satisfies
23                                                                                                    23

24                                      yi + L∥ x ′ − xi ∥1 ≤ f ( x ′ ) + ν.                          24

25                                                                                                    25

26
     The cone and the envelope are both L-Lipschitz. Hence                                            26

27                                                                                                    27
                                    yi + L∥ x − xi ∥1 − f ( x ) ≤ ν + 2Lr.
28                                                                                                    28

29   A feasible executed action within distance α of ai adds at most L a α to Q. This proves          29

30   the stated execution allowance. Rounding a rational switching boundary without a                 30

31   checked comparison can change the selected index; it must be covered by ν or by a                31

32   certified interval containing all eligible indices. An exact tie presents no difficulty,         32
                               NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                     21

1    since both attaining cones give the same certified upper bound. The current records                     1

2    store exact rational boundaries and define the ideal selector. They do not claim a                      2

3    measured deployment performance for arbitrary hardware.                                                 3

4                                                                                                            4

5                          10.4. Scalar compilation with original witnesses                                  5

6      Let 0 = x0 < · · · < x N = 1 and let the original numerical labels yi be arbitrary                    6

7    rational numbers. Define pairs                                                                          7

8                                                                                                            8

9           ( pi , li ) = min(y j + L( xi − x j ), j),      (qi , ri ) = min(y j + L( x j − xi ), j),        9
                        j ≤i                                             j ≥i
10                                                                                                           10
     where pairs are ordered first by value and then by index. A forward pass computes
11                                                                                                           11
     the first array by comparing (yi , i ) with ( pi−1 + L( xi − xi−1 ), li−1 ); a backward pass
12                                                                                                           12
     computes the second. The index always refers to an original label and its action, not
13                                                                                                           13
     to a newly regularized value with no action witness.
14                                                                                                           14
       On [ xi , xi+1 ], the envelope is the minimum of
15                                                                                                           15

16                             pi + L ( x − xi )    and    q i +1 + L ( x i +1 − x ),                        16

17                                                                                                           17

18
     with witnesses li and ri+1 . When L > 0 their intersection is                                           18

19                                             x i + x i +1 q i +1 − p i                                     19
                                        si =               +             .
20                                                  2            2L                                          20

21   Unlike an intersection computed after closing both labels, this intersection may lie                    21

22   outside the cell. In that case one affine candidate attains the minimum throughout                      22

23   the cell. An interior intersection divides the cell into exactly two witness regions. 23
24   If L = 0, compare the two constants directly. The implementation preserves every                        24

25   original node boundary and every interior switch, and chooses the right cell at an                      25

26   exact boundary. Continuity ensures that an adjoining witness remains attaining at                       26

27   the boundary. Thus there are at most 2N witness intervals, with rational endpoints                      27

28   and original action indices.                                                                            28

29     Once rational endpoints are loaded, an ordered search locates a region using                          29

30   a logarithmic number of comparisons. Reading and parsing all stored rational                            30

31   cuts anew costs linear work and is not removed by that comparison bound. The                            31

32   reference accessor used by the tests performs that parsing; the catalogue measures                      32
     22

1    construction and serialization, not repeated policy-query throughput. The record’s          1

2    binary-search count is only a count of ordered comparisons, not the complete cost           2

3    of that accessor. Rational numerator/denominator lengths, cuts, witness indices             3

4    and original actions are stored separately.                                                 4

5                                                                                                5

6                     10.5. A common strengthened nearest-node certificate                       6

7      For a nearest-node policy and any fitted piecewise-affine continuation, let               7

8                                                                                                8

9                            D = max sup {yi + L| x − xi | − f ( x )}.                           9
                                    i   x ∈Ci
10                                                                                               10

11
     The selected-node query and the state modulus give Q( x, π ( x )) − f ( x ) ≤ e + D. On     11

12
     each intersection of a nearest-node cell with a linearity interval of f , divided also at   12

13
     xi , the expression is affine. Its maximum is attained at an endpoint. Consequently it      13

14
     suffices to enumerate the union of the fitted knots, original state nodes and nearest- 14
15
     cell boundaries. At a shared nearest-cell boundary both possible node indices are           15

16
     included. This is an exact all-state calculation, rather than a sampled maximum.            16

17
       Both the cone-nearest ablation and spline-nearest comparator use this calculation         17

18
     and their own valid optimal-residual upper bounds. The former has D ≥ 0 because             18

19
     the cone envelope lies below every candidate cone. Its witness counterpart achieves         19

20
     zero for this particular label-transport upper account. This statement is about the         20

21
     account, not an optimality theorem for the actual policy among all feasible controls. 21
22
     The spline’s continuation is not replaced by the cone envelope and continues to             22

23
     propagate its own exact Lipschitz constant.                                                 23

24                                                                                               24
                        10.6. Evidence identities and scope of reproduction
25                                                                                               25

26     The protocol predates the complete 36-service execution. Fourteen new checks ex- 26
27   amine exact compilation, noisy and dominated labels, ties, finite-policy comparison,        27

28   shift cancellation, common nearest-cell bounds, exact continuous-uniform integra- 28
29   tion in small examples, and immutable numerical dependencies. They supplement               29

30   the analytic proof rather than replace it. The source hashes, all 216 checkpoints,          30

31   and complete process clocks are retained. Formula reconstruction verifies each one- 31
32   sided component, every first crossing, and the identity of the two neural critics in        32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                          23

1    the actor ablation. Three repetitions of a deterministic policy are not three economic   1

2    draws.                                                                                   2

3      The latest R43 supplemental report concerns a different, earlier incomplete snap- 3
4    shot. The current reply does not claim that its missing source parts were present. 4
5    It identifies the complete R46 source tree as the new review object and retains the      5

6    earlier review and incomplete historical branch unchanged. The predeclared R46           6

7    execution, R45 construction catalogue, R44 postselected diagnostics, original R42        7

8    capped services and original paired cost comparisons remain distinct evidence            8

1                 11. FEASIBLE TRANSPORT UNDER ENDOGENOUS CONSTRAINTS                               1

2                                                                                                   2
       We give the details underlying the feasible-witness transport theorem in the main
3
     article. All norms below are the specified ℓ1 norms. The evaluation functional is              3

4                                                                                                   4
     the same at the compared state–action pairs. Its monotonicity and cash invariance
5                                                                                                   5
     imply that changing its bounded random input by at most b changes its value by at
6                                                                                                   6
     most b. Thus the primitive state and action inequalities on the feasible graph imply
7                                                                                                   7

8                 | Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) − Qt ( f t+1 ; z, b)| ≤ AtQ ∥ x − z∥1 + DtQ ∥ a − b∥1        8

9                                                                                                   9
     whenever a ∈ At ( x ) and b ∈ At (z). The proof never evaluates Qt outside that graph.
10                                                                                                  10

11                                                                                                  11
                        11.1. The Bellman modulus and noisy feasible labels
12                                                                                                  12
       Fix a ∈ At ( x ). Compactness and the Hausdorff condition give b ∈ At (z) with
13                                                                                                  13
     ∥ a − b∥1 ≤ KtA ∥ x − z∥1 . It follows that
14                                                                                                  14

15                            Tt f t+1 (z) ≤ Qt ( f t+1 ; x, a) + Lt ∥ x − z∥1 .                    15

16                                                                                                  16

17
     Taking the infimum over a and then reversing x, z establishes the claimed Bellman              17

18
     modulus, without assuming that the minimizer is known. At node i, the minimum                  18

19
     of exact objective values over its feasible action net is at least the continuous feasible     19

20
     infimum and at most that infimum plus DtQ k t . Taking the minimum of queries with             20

21
     absolute error at most et adds at most et in each direction.                                   21

22
       For every cone, the lower label inequality and the Bellman modulus give                      22

23                                                                                                  23
                                yt,i + Lt ∥ x − xt,i ∥1 ≥ Tt f t+1 ( x ) − et .
24                                                                                                  24

25   For a nearest state node, the upper label inequality gives an upper bound                      25

26   Tt f t+1 ( x ) + DtQ k t + et + 2Lt ht . The finite minimum has the same bounds and is         26

27   Lt -Lipschitz. The next backward step therefore has precisely the modulus required             27

28   by the construction, independently of the numerical label errors.                              28

29                                                                                                  29

30
                           11.2. Repair, selection and the dynamic account                          30

31     The stored action at,i attains an original numerical query; hence its exact node             31

32   objective is at most yt,i + et . For the minimizing cone, the feasible-pair inequality         32
     26

1    and repair displacement give                                                                  1

2                                                                                                  2
                             Qt ( f t+1 ; x, Γt,i ( x )) − f t ( x ) ≤ et + DtQ ζ t .
3                                                                                                  3

4    A fixed ordering of finitely many continuous cone values gives a Borel index selector. 4
5    Composing the finitely many Borel repairs with that selector gives a feasible Borel           5

6    policy. The one-sided sandwich now supplies the discounted loss bound, with the               6

7    original terminal interval unchanged. The full-policy comparison is exactly the               7

8    Bellman comparison assumed in the controlled economy; it is not a comparison                  8

9    restricted to the node action arrays.                                                         9

10     The four nonterminal resource allocations make the bracket at most s/4 + s/4 + 10
11   s/4 + s/4 = s. The terminal bracket is at most s. Effective finite covers, feasible           11

12   action nets, query enclosures and repair procedures consequently give finite termi- 12
13   nation. Where only an approximate repair is computable, its certified displacement            13

14   is charged through DtQ ζ t and its actual action must still be feasible. A numerical          14

15   tolerance for an infeasible constraint violation is not included in this theorem.             15

16                                                                                                 16

17                                 11.3. A transparent exact check                                 17

18     Let X = [0, 1]2 , b( x ) = ( x1 + x2 )/2, A( x ) = [0, b( x )], and consider the one-date   18

19   objective                                                                                     19

20                                                                                                 20
                             Q( x, a) = ( a − 3/4)2 + x1 /8 + x2 /16.
21                                                                                                 21

22   Here AQ = 1/8, D Q = 3/2, K A = 1/2, and L = 7/8 are valid moduli. The exact                  22

23   optimum is attained at min{3/4, b( x )}. For a feasible anchor action, clipping at b( x )     23

24   changes the action by at most |b( x ) − b( xi )| ≤ ∥ x − xi ∥1 /2. The transport theorem      24

25   therefore applies directly, and a reference implementation can compare the exact              25

26   repaired-policy loss with the analytic bound using rational arithmetic.                       26

27     The additional tests independently form feasible node nets and noisy labels,                27

28   compute the cone minimum by direct enumeration, and check the Bellman and                     28

29   selected-policy enclosures on a fixed rational set. They also check the Hausdorff             29

30   modulus, exact feasibility, approximate-repair accounting, nonminimizing-index                30

31   allowances and the zero-constraint-variation reduction. The analytic proof above,             31

32   rather than a finite grid test, establishes the all-state claim. The tests are not training   32
                            NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                               27

1    data, timing observations, or evidence of a high-dimensional speed advantage. No                1

2    R46 catalogue record is altered by these checks.                                                2

                      12. ACQUIRED STATES AND IMPLEMENTABLE WITNESSES
5                                                                                                    5

6
       This section supplies the implementation and statistical details for the new                  6

7
     acquisition-aware results. The earlier proofs remain in force on their stated do- 7
8
     mains. To avoid conflating economic objects, the constrained study and the direct               8

9
     scalar comparison are separate experiments: the former constructs new two-state                 9

10
     policies, while the latter evaluates the actual scalar policies previously constructed. 10
11                                                                                                   11

12                                                                                                   12
                       12.1. Causal information and the one-sided comparison
13                                                                                                   13

14     Let Ht contain the primitive history and the observations available before date-t             14

15   action. The observation process is causal and preserves the stipulated conditional              15

16   law, or conditional recursive evaluator, for the next primitive innovation. This con- 16
17   dition permits state errors to depend arbitrarily on the available past. It does not            17

18   permit a sensor to reveal a future innovation without changing the economic infor- 18
19   mation structure. The full-information optimum is over feasible policies adapted to             19

20   this permitted history. Uniform one-step inequalities therefore apply to observation- 20
21   dependent actions as well as exact-state Markov selectors.                                      21

22     Suppose f t − Tt f t+1 ≤ ut , and suppose every permitted observation and true                22

23   state obey Ttπb f t+1 − f t ≤ dt . If ℓ T ≤ f T − g ≤ u T , induction gives                     23

24                                                                                                   24
                             T −1                                   T −1
                 Vt∗ ≥ f t − ∑ Bt,j u j − Bt,T u T ,    Jtπb ≤ f t + ∑ Bt,j d j − Bt,T ℓ T .
25                                                                                                   25

26                            j=t                                    j=t                             26

27                                                                                                   27

28   The second inequality is interpreted on Ht when the policy uses the observation his- 28
29   tory. Monotonicity and cash invariance justify every induction step on the common               29

30   evaluation domain. Subtraction yields the sum of u j + d j and terminal width; no               30

31   actor continuity is used. A date shift f t 7→ f t + st adds st − β t st+1 to ut and subtracts   31

32   the same quantity from dt . The policy-loss account is invariant.                               32
     28

11   Combining this with the optimal-residual upper bound DtQ k t + et + 2Lt ht proves               11

12   the main theorem. This proof charges an objective excess even when two selected                 12

13   actions differ substantially at a boundary.                                                     13

14     For a capacity interval, exact repair at the true state has displacement ( ai −               14

15   κ ( x ))+ ≤ K A ∥ x − xi ∥1 . Replacing capacity by a nonnegative certified lower value         15

16   changes the repaired action by at most κ ( x ) − κ ( xb). Downward quantization adds            16

17   less than ∆. These inequalities prove both feasibility and the stated displacement              17

18   bound. In the executed affine-capacity economy the acquisition box has coordinate               18

19   endpoints max(0, xbj − η ) and min(1, xbj + η ). Its minimum capacity is computed at            19

20   the lower corner. All these quantities are dyadic, so that the capacity and downward            20

21   action rounding are exact at the specified resolutions.                                         21

23                   12.2. Finite-precision indices and the identical envelope                       23

24     The scalar and two-state experiments store labels on a dyadic lattice. In the                 24

25   acquired-state deployment, nodes and measured coordinates have the declared                     25

26   dyadic denominators; their coordinate differences and the sum of two absolute                   26

27   differences are exactly representable in binary64. No overflow or underflow occurs              27

28   in the evaluated range. For a cone vi = yi + L∥ xb − xi ∥1 , let e
                                                                      L denote the binary64          28

29   slope and vei its computed score. The standard product-and-sum bound, with unit                 29

30   roundoff u = 2−53 , gives                                                                       30

31                                                                                                   31
                                                                       2u

                          L − L| + γ2 (Y + d|e
                                             L|) = τ,           γ2 =        ,       Y = max |yi |.

                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                          29

1    If i minimizes the computed scores and j minimizes the exact scores, then vi ≤            1

2    vei + τ ≤ vej + τ ≤ v j + 2τ. Thus ν = 2τ works regardless of a tie. More general state   2

3    formats must charge the formation of distances too; that cost is not suppressed by        3

4    this dyadic example.                                                                      4

5         An affine–ReLU minimum gate is a − ( a − b)+ . For exact inputs it equals the        5

6    native minimum. Attaching the same index and the same tie rule proves equality            6

7    of the witness actor, including feasible repair. The proof is algebraic and covers        7

8    all states, not only the measurement grid. The scalar implementation’s compiled           8

9    right-cell tie convention is held fixed in this control.                                  9

10        Ordinary rounded ReLU gates need not agree bit for bit with native minima. If        10

11   the exact intermediate minima have absolute value at most M, input errors are at          11

12   most E, and two subtractions implement one gate, a conservative next-level bound          12

13   is                                                                                        13

14                                                                                             14
                                     Enext ≤ E + 6u( M + E)
15                                                                                             15

16   under the preceding finite-range conditions. Indeed the first subtraction contributes     16

17   at most 2u( M + E), the positive-part map is nonexpansive, and the last subtraction       17

18   contributes at most u(3 + 2u)( M + E). The exact minimum is nonexpansive in               18

19   its two arguments. Iteration from the leaf conversion bound controls a balanced           19

20   circuit. The retained diagnostic checks all 24 scalar witness checkpoints on 1,025        20

21   dyadic points against this budget. Its gate-evaluation clock excludes construction        21

22   and is not a method-level speed comparison. The native representation and the             22

23   ReLU representation share parameters; they are not counted as independent trained         23

24   policies.                                                                                 24
