                 13. EXPLICIT CONSTRAINED PRIMITIVES AND RESOURCE ALLOCATION
26                                                                                             26

27        For the new two-state economy, the action lies in [0, 1/4]. Bounding the non- 27
28   negative state terms separately gives F1 ∈ [1/32, 29/32] and F2 ∈ [1/32, 27/32]. 28
29   These bounds already establish invariance before any numerical enclosure is in- 29
30   tersected with the domain. The two state-column sums of the transition Jacobian           30

31   are 11/16 − x2 /8 and 11/16 − x1 /8; every term used in these sums is nonneg- 31
32   ative. The induced ℓ1 state modulus is therefore 11/16. The action column has             32
     30

1    norm 1/2 + 1/4 = 3/4. Capacity has gradient (1/16, 1/16) and hence ℓ1 Lipschitz           1

2    constant 1/16.                                                                            2

3      Let s = (1/2 − x1 − x2 )+ . The first state derivative of stage cost is 2( x1 − 5/8) + 3
4    ( x1 − x2 )/2 − 4s; the second is the symmetric expression. On each of the two short- 4
5    age regions these derivatives are affine. At the vertices of the resulting polygons       5

6    their absolute values are at most 13/4. Doubling the squared-target part changes          6

7    this bound to 9/2 for terminal cost. The action derivative 2pa + 16a3 is nonnega- 7
8    tive and at most p/2 + 1/4. These are constants for the primitive equations, not          8

9    estimates from the evaluation grid.                                                       9

10     For a uniform tensor grid, each coordinate is within 1/(2N ) of a node, giving          10

11   h = 1/N. The maximum feasible interval length is 1/4, so a uniform N-subdivision          11

12   feasible net has radius at most 1/(8N ). At different states the capacity intervals       12

13   have Hausdorff distance at most (1/16)∥ x − x ′ ∥1 . The map a 7→ min{ a, κ ( x )} is     13

14   measurable and feasible, with the displacement bound proved above. Thus neither           14

15   an unspecified covering oracle nor an unspecified feasible projection is used here.       15

16     A shock cell has length 1/(16M). Its conditional mean distance to the midpoint is       16

17   1/(64M ). The transition’s shock derivative is (1, −1), of ℓ1 norm two. Consequently      17

18   a future continuation of modulus L has midpoint expectation error at most L/(32M). 18
19   The program encloses numerical evaluation at all midpoints, averages outward,             19

20   adds this continuous-law remainder, and discounts it. Labels are dyadic rounded           20

21   centers with an error enclosing the full numerical query. The economic law remains        21

22   continuous; this is not a proof for a substituted finite-state shock process.             22

23                                                                                             23

24                           13.1. The strengthened bilinear comparator                        24

25                                                                                             25
       Write λi ( x ) for the four bilinear interpolation weights in a tensor cell. They are
26                                                                                             26
     nonnegative, sum to one and reproduce the two coordinates. In one coordinate,
27                                                                                             27
     at relative position q ∈ [0, 1], the weighted distance to the two endpoints is 2q(1 −
28                                                                                             28
     q)/N ≤ 1/(2N ). Adding coordinates gives ∑i λi ( x )∥ x − xi ∥1 ≤ 1/N. If the nodal
     Bellman labels have lower error −et and upper error DtQ k t + et , the graph-Lipschitz
29                                                                                             29

30                                                                                             30
     Bellman target therefore obeys
31                                                                                             31

32                    −et − Lt /N ≤ f t ( x ) − Tt f t+1 ( x ) ≤ DtQ k t + et + Lt /N.         32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                            31

1    The interpolant’s own Lipschitz modulus is N times the largest adjacent nodal              1

2    difference in absolute value. Each partial derivative is a convex combination of two       2

3    adjacent differences. In this execution the differences are dyadic with sufficiently few   3

4    significant bits to be represented exactly; the computed modulus is consequently           4

5    an upper bound without a hidden downward rounding.                                         5

6      For the nearest-node actor, each closed Voronoi cell is split at its node coordi- 6
7    nates. On each of the at most four resulting rectangles the absolute-distance term is      7

8    affine, and the continuation is a single bilinear polynomial. Fixing one coordinate        8

9    makes the difference affine in the other. Successively maximizing both coordinates         9

10   therefore attains a maximum at a vertex. The union contains at most nine points. 10
11   Evaluating every point outward bounds the complete supremum, including bound- 11
12   ary ties. This proves the selected-policy allowance used by the comparator rather          12

13   than comparing a sharpened witness bound to an unnecessarily separated baseline. 13
14                                                                                              14

15                          13.2. Sufficient caps versus executed caps                          15

16     For the cone generator,                                                                  16

17                                                                                              17
                                          A                 A
18                           Lt = Cx + K Ca + β( Fx + K Fa ) Lt+1 .                             18

19                                                                                              19
     Here b = β( Fx + K A Fa ) = 705/1024 < 1, giving the horizon-uniform bound L =
20                                                                                              20
     max{ L g , (Cx + K A Ca )/(1 − b)}. The action modulus is at most D = Ca + βFa L.
21                                                                                              21
     With M = N, nonterminal label error is at most βL/(32N ) + ξ. Substituting into the
22                                                                                              22
     one-sided loss bound yields exactly
23                                                                                              23
                                                        T
24                        ST ( D/8 + 2L + βL/16) + 2β L g                                       24
                   GN ≤                                   + 2ST ξ + 2β T ξ T .
25                                       N                                                      25

26   Choosing the spatial and numerical allocations as in the article proves the suffi- 26
27   cient cap. It is not a statement that the fixed ladder 4, 8, 16 contains that cap, nor     27

28   that the labels rounded to a fixed lattice support arbitrary tolerances. Acquisition,      28

29   quantization and selector excess are additional summands in the same budget.               29

30     There are ( N + 1)2 nodes, N + 1 feasible actions per node, N shock midpoints            30

31   per query and T backward dates. A direct cone query evaluates ( N + 1)2 pieces,            31

32   while a bilinear query evaluates one local patch. The resulting operation bounds           32
     32

1    are O( TN 6 ) and O( TN 4 ), respectively. These formulas include label formation,            1

2    not only verification. Actor storage and critic-label storage are O( TN 2 ). Circuit          2

3    compilation, cell verification, selector queries, feasible repair, numerical enclosure,       3

4    coefficient conversion and serialization are separately counted or bounded in the             4

5    stated implementation. Arbitrary precision multiplies arithmetic counts by the                5

6    cost of the chosen bit operations and requires enough guard precision to meet the             6

7    query error. These are upper bounds for the specified routines, not lower bounds              7

8    against alternative algorithms. In general dimension the original state and action            8

9    covering factors remain visible. No computational saving follows from relabeling              9

10   the identical envelope as a neural circuit.                                                   10

11                                                                                                 11
              14. DIRECT POLICY VALUES WITH NUMERICAL AND SAMPLING ERROR
12                                                                                                 12

13                             14.1. Identity and deterministic support                            13

14     For a fixed policy m and its own fitted continuation, let                                   14

15                                                                                                 15

16               Zm = f 0m ( X0 ) + BT ( g( XT ) − f Tm ( XT ))                                    16

17                      + ∑ Bt {ct ( Xt , Am              m                       m
                                           t ) + β t E[ f t+1 ( Xt+1 ) | Ht ] − f t ( Xt )}.
                                                                                                   17

18                         t< T                                                                    18

19   Conditional expectation and telescoping show EZm = Jm for either a fixed or ran- 19
20   dom initial state. Policies in a pair may be driven by common shocks; preservation            20

21   of each marginal law is sufficient. The innovation dependence between the two                 21

22   policies changes variance, not this identity.                                                 22

23     For f tm − Tt f tm+1 ≤ um
                               t and Tt
                                        πm m
                                          f t+1 − f tm ≤ dm
                                                          t , the realized conditional residual    23

24   is in [−um    m
              t , dt ]. The lower bound follows because a feasible action cannot be below          24

25   the Bellman infimum. Adding the ranges of these residuals, the terminal differences,          25

26   and the initial difference gives the deterministic support in the article. For a common       26

27   random initial state the initial difference is piecewise affine on the union of the           27

28   two knot sets; extrema occur at those knots. For fixed initial states it is evaluated         28

29   exactly as a rational number. Initial randomness is therefore not replaced by an              29

30   unsupported average over four hand-selected points.                                           30

31     The frozen scalar terminal allowance is reconstructed from its stored width. If             31

32   terminal node error is eT , a cone continuation gives g − f T ∈ [−eT − 2L g h T , eT ], and   32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                33

1    a spline gives g − f T ∈ [−eT − L g h T , eT + L g h T ]. All signs are retained when forming   1

2    pair support. Subtracting separate regret bounds would not give this estimand.                  2

3                                                                                                    3

4                        14.2. Bins, discontinuities, and endpoint statistics                        4

5                                                                                                    5
       Each independent uniform integer specifies one of 240 equal innovation bins,
6                                                                                                    6
     and, for the uniform initial-state law, one such initial bin. The lower and upper
7                                                                                                    7
     path computation encloses every continuous point of that bin. Conditional uniform
8
     randomization within each independent bin recovers the original continuous uni- 8
9                                                                                                    9
     form law. It need not be explicitly generated because every such value is already
10                                                                                                   10
     enclosed.
11                                                                                                   11
       At a selector boundary, stored rational cuts are enclosed outward. Binary search
12                                                                                                   12
     obtains the first and last cells intersecting the state interval. The action interval is the
13                                                                                                   13
     hull of all actions in those cells, not merely its two endpoint actions. Propagating
14                                                                                                   14
     the hull may be conservative but is valid even for a nonmonotone actor. No path is
15                                                                                                   15
     removed. Intersections with the proved invariant state domain and deterministic
16                                                                                                   16
     score support preserve enclosure. The stored ambiguity counts quantify this part of
17                                                                                                   17
     the computation. These endpoint observations are independent across paths under
18                                                                                                   18
     the stated sampling model, even though endpoints and contrasts within a path are
19                                                                                                   19
     dependent.
20                                                                                                   20
       Here are the numerical details of the empirical Bernstein calculation. For a
21
     binary64 endpoint sample x1 , . . . , xn ∈ [ a, b], let M = max(| a|, |b|) and γ = (n + 21
22                                                                                                   22
     4)u/(1 − (n + 4)u). Denote the computed mean and mean square by µ
                                                                     e, qe. The exact
23                                                                                                   23
     moments obey
24                                                                                                   24
                                                                   2            2
25                    µ ∈ [µ
                           e − γM, µ
                                   e + γM],          q ∈ [qe − γM , qe + γM ].                       25

26                                                                                                   26
     The bound allows sequential summation, multiplication and division and is conser-
27                                                                                                   27
     vative for the implementation’s pairwise sums. Rational arithmetic constructs these
28                                                                                                   28
     intervals from the exact binary64 outputs. There is no uncharged empirical-variance
29                                                                                                   29
     rounding. If [µ− , µ+ ] is the mean enclosure, then
30                                                                                                   30
                                                                
31
                                   +     n                     2                                     31
                                  V =          q+ − min v
32                                     n−1         v∈[µ− ,µ+ ]     +                                 32
     34

1    is an upper bound for the unbiased sample variance. The minimum is zero if the          1

2    mean interval contains zero and otherwise the smaller squared endpoint.                 2

3      There are K = 120 contrasts, each with two one-sided tails, and total error α =       3

4    0.01. The bound of Maurer and Pontil (2009) uses log(4K/α) = log(48000) < 11. 4
5    The inequality is verified by the exact positive sum ∑40    j
                                                           j=0 11 /j! > 48000. A valid       5

6    endpoint radius is                                                                      6

7                                                                                            7
                                         p               7(b − a) 11
8                           R (V + ) =       2V + 11/n +             .                       8
                                                          3( n − 1)
9                                                                                            9

10   The code rounds the square-root result upward until its exact rational square is at     10

11   least the radicand. All remaining additions are rational. Subtract this radius from     11

12   the lower endpoint’s mean lower bound and add it to the upper endpoint’s mean           12

13   upper bound. Intersect with known support, then round the final reported display        13

14   outward. This proves simultaneous coverage with probability at least 0.99 condi- 14
15   tional on the fixed-policy, independent-bin model. A seed supplies reproducibility,     15

16   not evidence that a pseudorandom generator satisfies a mathematical independence        16

17   axiom.                                                                                  17

18                                                                                           18

19                                                                                           19
                             14.3. Decision margins and selection
20                                                                                           20

21     The protocol fixed the superiority threshold at zero and the separate deci- 21
22   sion tolerance at 1/1024 before the new comparison. Thus a full interval inside         22

23   [−1/1024, 1/1024] establishes two-sided cost proximity in the normalized units          23

24   on the joint coverage event. It does not establish equality, a signed ranking, or a     24

25   calibrated welfare equivalence. The theorem for choosing among a finite catalogue       25

26   is a direct inequality on this same event: Jmb − Jb ≤ Umb and Um ≤ Jm − Jb + Um − Lm . 26
27   Minimizing Um + k m gives the stated oracle inequality. Charges are known inputs to     27

28   that decision problem; converting observed seconds to an economic charge requires       28

29   an additional model and is not performed here.                                          29

30     The complete cost intervals follow. The same fixed policy may appear under            30

31   distinct target labels; its identity is retained in every row. A timing repetition is   31

32   not a new trained policy. All lower and upper endpoint moments, supports, seeds,        32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                    35

1    stream hashes, ambiguity counts and input checkpoint hashes are deposited with     1

2    the result records.                                                                2

9      W is the witness, C the same-critic nearest actor, and S the spline. Every displayed    9

10   endpoint is rounded outward to seven decimal places; the exact rational intervals         10

11   control all decisions and are deposited. All 120 intervals lie inside the predeclared     11

12   band. Repeated target labels and policy hashes remain explicit in the underlying          12

13   records.                                                                                  13

15
                        15. COMPLETE FRONTIERS AND EXPERIMENT RECORDS                          15

16     For each method, the first-crossing function is constant between adjacent values        16

17   of its recorded certified bounds. Partitioning the positive tolerance axis by the union   17

18   of both methods’ bounds therefore gives the exact comparison everywhere, with left        18

19   endpoints included because attainment is weak inequality. The full-prefix Bellman         19

20   count charges every earlier failed rung. Above the largest breakpoint both methods        20

21   use their first rung. An interval in which only one method attains is distinguished       21

22   from an interval in which both attain with different query counts. This descriptive       22

23   reconstruction is not a new experimental target design.                                   23

           16. CONTINUOUS TENSOR COMPILATION AND FEASIBLE WITNESS IDENTITY
30                                                                                            30

31     This section proves Theorem 14 of the article. We state its identities explicitly      31

32   so that the numerical compiler can be checked without relying on an unproved             32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                 43

1    interpolation rule. The original labels and original witness indices, not transformed- 1
2    node actions, are the inputs.                                                                      2

3                                                                                                       3

4                                                                                                       4
                             16.1. The line transform and its tie invariant
5                                                                                                       5
       For ordered line locations s0 < · · · < sm , attach to each location a pair (q j , a j ),
6                                                                                                       6
     where q j is a real cost and a j an original index. Order such pairs lexicographically.
7                                                                                                       7
     Addition of a scalar to a pair adds it only to the cost. Start with u j = (q j , a j ) and
8                                                                                                       8
     perform
9                                                                                                       9

10                                                                                                      10
                u j ← min{u j , u j−1 + L(s j − s j−1 )},                 j = 1, . . . , m,       (1)
11                     lex                                                                              11

12              u j ← min{u j , u j+1 + L(s j+1 − s j )},                 j = m − 1, . . . , 0.         12
                       lex
13                                                                                                      13

14   After the forward pass, induction gives u j = mink≤ j,lex (qk + L(s j − sk ), ak ). The            14

15   backward pass makes u j the minimum over all original line inputs of (qk + L|s j −                 15

16   sk |, ak ). To see this directly, a site to the right can be reached by the monotone               16

17   backward path; a site to the left has already been considered in the forward pass. 17
18   Any path that reverses direction has weakly larger distance because L ≥ 0, so it                   18

19   cannot improve the cost. With L = 0 the original smallest index propagates in                      19

20   both directions. Lexicographic addition and minimum preserve the tie information                   20

21   throughout.                                                                                        21

22     For a tensor grid, define the coordinate operator Tj by taking this minimum along                22

23   coordinate j with all other coordinates fixed. Expanding the finite minima shows                   23

24                                                                                                      24

25                           Td · · · T1 (yi , i )(v) = min(yi + L∥v − xi ∥1 , i ).                     25
                                                     i,lex
26                                                                                                      26

27   Thus the final result is (zv , ov ) in (48). Each grid edge is relaxed at most twice in            27

28   each coordinate. The edge count is at most dS, and all updates can be made in place                28

29   with one cost and one index per node. The separable distance-transform part is                     29

30   the classical algorithm; see Felzenszwalb and Huttenlocher (2012). Original action                 30

31   owners are auxiliary information propagated by the same minimum operations,                        31

32   not independently recomputed choices.                                                              32
     44

1                        16.2. Off-grid extension and the original tie rule                         1

2                                                                                                   2
       For any corner v and any original site i, the triangle inequality gives
3                                                                                                   3

4                       y i + L ∥ x − x i ∥1 ≤ y i + L ∥ v − x i ∥1 + L ∥ x − v ∥1 .                4

5                                                                                                   5

6    Minimizing first over i proves the lower inequality in (49). For the converse, fix             6

7    a site attaining f ( x ). If its jth coordinate is at or to the left of the cell, choose the   7

8    left corner coordinate; if it is at or to the right, choose the right corner coordinate. 8
9    A grid site cannot lie strictly between adjacent grid coordinates. When x lies on              9

10   the cell boundary, either admissible corner choice gives the same needed identity. 10
11   Summing the coordinate identities gives                                                        11

12                                                                                                  12

13
                                ∥ x − x i ∥1 = ∥ x − v ∥1 + ∥ v − x i ∥1 .                          13

14                                                                                                  14
     Using this site in zv proves the upper inequality and hence equality.
15                                                                                                  15
       Now take the smallest attaining original index i∗ ( x ). For its associated corner v,
16                                                                                                  16
     equality in the preceding chain implies zv = yi∗ + L∥v − xi∗ ∥1 . If the original owner
17                                                                                                  17
     ov were smaller than i∗ , then
18                                                                                                  18

19                        y o v + L ∥ x − x o v ∥1 ≤ z v + L ∥ x − v ∥1 = f ( x ).                  19

20                                                                                                  20

21   It would be an attaining original site with a smaller index, a contradiction. Therefore        21

22   ov = i∗ ( x ). Evaluating the original cones of all corner owners and taking their lexi- 22
23   cographic minimum proves (50). This argument covers inconsistent or dominated                  23

24   labels: it never uses zv = yv .                                                                24

25     Attaching the original action aov to a transformed label is essential. Attaching av          25

26   need not preserve the policy. Under the established identity, both implementations             26

27   select i∗ ( x ) and apply the same feasible repair Γi∗ ( x ). They consequently induce         27

28   the same action at every state, the same controlled transition law and, by backward            28

29   evaluation, the same policy value. The argument is not restricted to expected                  29

30   costs; identity of the actual policy preserves any well-defined common recursive               30

31   evaluation. It does not equate two policies generated from different rounded label             31

32   arrays.                                                                                        32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             45

1                          16.3. Finite arithmetic and uncertain queries                          1

2                                                                                                 2
       All preprocessing inputs in the executable study are exact rational representations
3                                                                                                 3
     of the stored numerical labels, slopes and coordinate knots. Comparisons in (1) are
4                                                                                                 4
     exact. The compiler stores the resulting rational zv and integer ov . Its arithmetic-
5                                                                                                 5
     operation count does not suppress numerator/denominator growth: coefficient bit
6                                                                                                 6
     lengths and actual preprocessing time are recorded.
7                                                                                                 7
       At an exact represented point, outward arithmetic encloses each term of (49);
8                                                                                                 8
     taking the minimum lower endpoint and the minimum upper endpoint encloses
9                                                                                                 9
     the true minimum. At an uncertain point x ∈ D, a midpoint value plus and minus
10                                                                                                10
     L supx∈ D ∥ x − xb∥1 is also a valid value enclosure. This is the continuation’s Lipschitz
11                                                                                                11
     property, not an actor regularity assertion.
12                                                                                                12
       For the actor, let C( D ) be the finitely many tensor cells whose closures meet a
13                                                                                                13
     query box. The previous proof implies that every exact winning original index at
14                                                                                                14
     any point in that box belongs to {ov : v ∈ V (C ), C ∈ C( D )}. An interval lower bound
15                                                                                                15
     above the smallest available upper bound rules out a candidate; all other candidates
16                                                                                                16
     are retained. Their action hull includes the exact actor at every permitted state.
17                                                                                                17
     Repair, downward action quantization, costs and transitions are then evaluated by
18                                                                                                18
     interval extensions. This may enlarge the path enclosure, but cannot improve the
19                                                                                                19
     statistical comparison by hiding a discontinuity. A degenerate ambiguous box is
20                                                                                                20
     resolved by exact original-cone comparison with the same smallest-index tie rule.
21                                                                                                21
       The off-grid work is bounded by the corner count only for a point or one-cell
22                                                                                                22
     query. A large uncertainty box can intersect many cells and requires their union. We
23                                                                                                23
     do not use the point-query complexity theorem to claim dimension-independent or
24                                                                                                24
     uncertainty-independent simulation work. The actual ambiguity and actor-query
25                                                                                                25
     counts are included in the direct-comparison records.
26                                                                                                26

27                                                                                                27
                          16.4. Work to implemented economic accuracy
28                                                                                                28

29     For Corollary 15, the deterministic primitive account gives G ≤ CT,p /N + 2ST ξ + 29
30   2β T ξ T . Under its allocations the first term is at most ε/4 and the two numerical terms   30

31   sum to at most ε/4. The exact-selector acquisition addition is A2−b + K∆ ≤ ε/2, so           31

32   the implemented loss is at most ε. A sufficient integer bit count can be found by            32
     46

1    increasing b until the rational inequality holds; no unchecked logarithm is required. 1
2    If observation has a fee, the augmented objective also pays λdbST . One cannot send               2

3    b to infinity at fixed positive price and infer a vanishing augmented loss.                       3

4      There are ( N + 1)2 state nodes, N + 1 actions at each node and N innovation                    4

5    midpoints. With a compiled continuation each midpoint requires a fixed number of                  5

6    two-dimensional primitive and corner operations, while transforming each date’s                   6

7    labels and storing its feasible actors requires O( N 2 ) further algebraic work. Thus the         7

8    full backward construction has O( TN 4 ) arithmetic operations. The dense alternative             8

9    multiplies the midpoint workload by a further ( N + 1)2 . The finite-bit actor applies            9

10   the same compiled owner rule and a constant-dimensional capacity repair. For a                    10

11   dyadic ladder ending at N, ∑ j≥0 ( N/2 j )q ≤ N q /(1 − 2−q ) for q > 0; this charges all         11

12   previous construction attempts. The storage of the entire ladder of checkpoints also              12

13   has the same O( TN 2 ) scalar order.                                                              13

14     These are sufficient algebraic upper bounds, not lower bounds for conventional                  14

15   methods. A p-bit elementary operation is charged at its actual or separately bounded              15

16   arithmetic cost, and exact rational owners carry their coefficient sizes. Statistical             16

17   policy comparison is outside this construction bound and is charged as its own                    17

18   joint service. No physical fsync latency, unknown optimizer iteration count, or                   18

19   unrestricted uncertainty-box enumeration is hidden inside the arithmetic order.                   19

20                                                                                                     20

1      Deploy the original action at the nearest coordinatewise node, choosing the                       1

2    smaller original index on a tie, with the same state-feasible repair. On its closed                 2

3    nearest-node cell Di , feasible objective transport gives                                           3

7                                                                                                        7

8                                                                                                        8

9                                                                                                        9

10                                                                                                       10

11                                                                                                       11

     The witness criterion remains (41) with exact repair. Both compare against the
17                                                                                                       17
     original optimum over all admissible adapted policies. Neither treats a sampled
18                                                                                                       18
     residual as an all-state error.
19                                                                                                       19
       The interpolant’s ℓ1 Lipschitz constant is the largest absolute edge slope: each
20                                                                                                       20
     partial derivative is a convex combination of edge slopes, and the dual norm of ℓ1
21                                                                                                       21

22                                                                                                       22
     not a neural modulus borrowed from another generator, is passed to that method’s
23                                                                                                       23

                         17.2. The prespecified error-driven coordinate grid
26                                                                                                       26

27     The adaptive conventional comparator is a nonuniform tensor FVI method, not                       27

28   a sparse grid or locally adaptive quadtree. At every date and resolution N > 4, it                  28

29   computes a fresh 5d pilot array using its own future continuation and the same N                    29

30   feasible action fractions and N innovation midpoints. At terminal date it evaluates                 30

31   the terminal primitive instead. These evaluations are charged even when a later                     31

32   grid repeats a pilot node.                                                                          32
     48

1      For coordinate j, let D j,k , k = 1, 2, 3, be the maximum, across all other pilot coor- 1
2    dinates, of the absolute second difference |yk−1 − 2yk + yk+1 |. Give coarse interval               2

3    [k/4, (k + 1)/4], k = 0, . . . , 3, weight                                                          3

4                                                                                                        4

5                             w j,k = 1 + 64 max{ D j,max(1,k) , D j,min(3,k+1) }.                       5

6                                                                                                        6
     Start with the four coarse intervals. Repeatedly bisect the interval maximizing w j,k
7                                                                                                        7
     times its squared length, breaking ties at the leftmost interval, until there are N
8                                                                                                        8
     intervals on that axis. All rankings and knots are rational. This positive curvature
9                                                                                                        9
     heuristic and its factor 64 were fixed in the scientific source before the full catalogue;
10                                                                                                       10
     no reported outcome selects them. The final cover uses the actual largest gaps. In
11                                                                                                       11
     particular, preferentially refining one area can worsen a global covering allowance
12                                                                                                       12
     elsewhere. The common certificate exposes that tradeoff rather than crediting the
13                                                                                                       13
     adaptive method with an assumed uniform radius.
14                                                                                                       14

1                   18. OBSERVATION CHARGES AND DIRECT COST INTERVALS                          1

2                                                                                              2
                           18.1. The sensor account and integer choice
3                                                                                              3

4      Each observed coordinate cell has width 2−b and midpoint error at most 2−(b+1) ,        4

5    including the boundary cells. For the affine capacity, evaluating the lower endpoint      5

6    of the observation box produces a feasible lower capacity. Its distance from the          6

7    true capacity is at most 2K A r (b). Downward action rounding by ∆ adds at most           7

8    ∆ to repair displacement. Therefore the acquisition addition at date t is 2Lt r (b) + 8
9    DtQ (2KtA r (b) + ∆t ). Summing discounted additions and adding the deterministic         9

10   observation charge gives (59). The same summation does not claim that a reference         10

11   policy-loss bound is a policy-cost point estimate.                                        11

12     The integer precision comparisons follow from the successive difference C −             12

13   A2−(b+1) . If this difference vanishes, both b and b + 1 minimize locally, and mono-      13

14   tonicity makes them global minimizers; the smaller is recorded. Every candidate           14

15   precision, charge, allowance and augmented bound is stored as a rational num- 15
16   ber. Checking the two neighbors, or all candidates in the finite range, is an exact       16

17   optimization certificate for this upper-bound objective.                                  17

18                                                                                             18

19                                                                                             19
                             18.2. Continuous-law path enclosures
20                                                                                             20

21     For a uniform initial law, two independent forty-bit bins cover the initial co- 21
22   ordinates. For a fixed initial vector, the coordinate inputs are exact. At each date      22

23   a further independent bin encloses the common continuous innovation. The bin              23

24   width is 2−40 for an initial coordinate and 2−44 for the shock interval of total length   24

25   1/16.                                                                                     25

26     For each controller separately, its state box determines all possible observation       26

27   bins; exact midpoints of those bins form an observation enclosure. The exact actor        27

28   at every possible observation is enclosed using original witness owners or the            28

29   nearest-node FVI cells. Capacity repair uses the lowest capacity on each possible         29

30   observation box. Its action hull includes all possible repaired and quantized actions. 30
31   The natural interval extensions of cost and transition then propagate the enclosure       31

32   to the next date. The compiler may accelerate these evaluations but never replaces a      32
                             NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                              51

1    path box by an unchecked midpoint actor. Dependence between the two policies is                1

2    arbitrary inside a paired path; their marginals retain the prescribed innovation law. 2
3         The running-cost support follows from the separate bounds ∑ j ( x j − 5/8)2 ≤             3

4    25/32, ( x1 − x2 )2 /4 ≤ 1/4, 2(1/2 − x1 − x2 )2+ ≤ 1/2, pa2 ≤ p/16, and 4a4 ≤ 1/64. 4
5    Terminal cost is at most 37/16. These inequalities prove (62); separate maxima need            5

6    not occur at the same state. The wider support is retained as a conservative input,            6

7    rather than estimated from the simulated sample.                                               7

8                                                                                                   8

9                                                                                                   9
                               18.3. Moment and probability accounts
10                                                                                                  10

11        For a bounded endpoint array X1 , . . . , Xn ∈ [ a, b], write M = max(| a|, |b|), u =     11

12   2−53 and γ = (n + 4)u/(1 − (n + 4)u). The implementation encloses the computed                 12

13   mean by adding and subtracting γM; it enlarges the computed mean square by                     13

14   γM2 . These conservative bounds cover multiplication, summation and division in                14

15   the finite arrays used here, with no overflow. If the mean enclosure is [m− , m+ ] and         15

16   the second-moment upper bound is q+ , an unbiased sample-variance upper bound                  16

17   is                                                                                             17

18                                                                                              18
                                                n
19                          s2+ = max       0,           q+ −      min        m   2
                                                                                           .        19
                                               n−1              m∈[m− ,m+ ]
20                                                                                                  20

21   The minimum square is zero exactly when the interval contains zero. All moment                 21

22   bounds are converted to rationals before computing the confidence allowance. A                 22

23   square-root upper endpoint is checked by exact squaring and rounded upward until               23

24   valid. The rational partial sum ∑40    j
                                      j=0 10 /j! > 19200 = 4K/α verifies the logarithmic            24

25   allowance ℓ = 10.                                                                              25

26        The empirical-Bernstein inequality of Maurer and Pontil (2009), applied to the            26

27   two bounded endpoint samples, gives (63)–(64). Each of the 2K one-sided asser- 27
28   tions receives failure probability α/(2K ). The union bound requires no indepen- 28
29   dence across policy-pair tasks, but each task’s endpoint sample uses the stipulated            29

30   independent-bin model. Stored PCG64 seeds and stream hashes give deterministic                 30

31   reproducibility only. The path enclosures are deterministic numerical statements;              31

32   the confidence level is a separate sampling statement under that model.                        32
     52

1      For an installed FVI controller, changing to witness costs k + Dµ,b . For an installed   1

2    witness controller, changing to FVI costs k − Dµ,b . The sign tests and no-recoupment      2

3    decision in Theorem 20 follow. They concern the chosen transaction fee and initial         3

4    law. They neither establish welfare equivalence under an unmodelled calibration            4

5    nor prove that both controllers are close to the optimum merely because they are           5

6    close to each other.                                                                       6

                            19. FULL WORK RECORDS AND PRESERVATION
8                                                                                               8

9      For each completed service, let gr be its exact bound at rung r and wr its accu- 9
10   mulated clock through that rung’s durable checkpoint. The first-crossing work at           10

11   tolerance ε is wmin{r:gr ≤ε} , with no attainment when this set is empty. Sorting the      11

12   union of all exact bound breakpoints yields a complete partition of the positive           12

13   tolerance axis into left-closed, right-open intervals on which every method’s first        13

14   crossing is fixed. Bounds need not be monotone for this construction: the earliest         14

15   qualifying rung is always used. The lowest interval with no attainment and the             15

16   unbounded upper interval are retained.                                                     16

17     Every interval in the machine-readable frontier contains each method’s selected          17

18   resolution, median/minimum/maximum prefix wall time, CPU time, component                   18

19   counts and durable checkpoint size. The three repetitions are deterministic con- 19
20   structions with separate process clocks; they are not independent economic tasks           20

21   or neural initializations. The dimension stress cells have one execution and are           21

22   labelled accordingly. Peak resident memory includes the interpreter and warm-up. 22
23   The internal prefix starts at primitives after the fixed non-economic warm-up; the         23

24   parent process records the startup-inclusive duration separately. CPU affinity and         24

25   numerical-library threads are fixed, while CPU frequency is not controlled.                25

26     The service includes target generation, adaptive pilots, exact compilation,              26

27   continuous-law integration and its remainder, actor extraction, certification, failed      27

28   rungs, serialization and a fixed finite acquired-deployment checksum. That check- 28
29   sum is a measured deployment component, not an empirical substitute for the                29

30   uniform acquisition theorem. An independent direct policy-cost experiment is a             30

31   separate joint service. Its clock includes both frozen-policy loads and compilation,       31

32   hash checks, common paths, repair, statistics and durable output. This pair-specific       32
                              NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                                        53

1    cost is reported separately; it is not assigned silently to one controller or called                      1

2    zero. Historical R46 scalar prefix-time curves are reconstructed from their original                      2

3    records without retiming or adding them to R48 clocks.                                                    3

4      The scalar, original constrained and precision findings remain historical evi- 4
5    dence. The new compiled construction is a separate prospective service, while the                         5

6    fixed-object compiler diagnostic retains the old labels and original witnesses. This                      6

7    separation prevents faster evaluation of a frozen object from being misreported as                        7

8    better optimizer reliability or a newly superior policy.                                                  8

9                                                                                                              9
                          20. EVERY DIRECT CONSTRAINED-POLICY INTERVAL

       Intervals concern witness minus FVI expected resource cost. The fee column                              11

12
     records the two-direction no-recoupment decision at k = 1/64. The familywise                              12

13
     statement is conditional on the independent-bin model. Displayed endpoints are                            13

14
     rounded outward.                                                                                          14

       Each row is a left-closed, right-open tolerance interval. At the exact rational                         20

24

      Counts are tolerance intervals, not probabilities or weighted economic tasks. W is the R46 witness
30    and S its spline comparator. These are unaltered historical internal prefix clocks, not R48 timings;       30

31    they are never added to new compiler or direct-comparison clocks. The exact partitions, selected           31

32    checkpoints and all three repeated times are retained.                                                     32

1                                 CURRENT R52 DEVELOPMENT                                     1

2                                                                                             2

3                         22. PRIMITIVE DOMAIN AND EXACT MODULI                               3

4                                                                                             4

5      Use the family in main equations (112)–(113), with d = 2, 3, 4. Capacity is between    5

6    1/8 and 1/4 and has ℓ1 modulus 1/(8d). Every component of the dynamics is at             6

7    least 1/32. A direct upper bound is 29/32 for the larger action exposure and 27/32       7

8    for the smaller one. Thus the cube is invariant without an action-dependent exit         8

9    event. The numerical clipping to [0, 1] is inactive on the true primitive image; it      9

10   does not authorize out-of-domain evaluation.                                             10

11     For d ≥ 2, the sums of the absolute entries in a state column of the transition        11

12   derivative are bounded by 1/2 + 1/16 + 1/8 = 11/16. The action derivative has ℓ1         12

13   norm ∑ j γ j . The action derivative of current cost is 2pa + 16a3 ≤ p/2 + 1/4. These    13

14   are bounds between feasible pairs, not a claim that the same action is feasible at       14

15   both states.                                                                             15

16     On either shortage region the state gradient is affine. The derivative of the          16

17   squared-target and imbalance terms in coordinate j, after multiplication by d, is        17

18   4( x j − 5/8) + (2x j − x j−1 − x j+1 )/2. In the shortage region add −8(1/2 − 2 ∑ x/d). 18
19   The terminal target derivative replaces 4 by 8. A linear functional on each region       19

20   reaches its extrema at region vertices. For the declared dimensions, these are cube      20

21   vertices and vertices with all but at most one coordinate at zero or one and ∑ x = d/4. 21
22   Exact evaluation gives absolute derivative bounds 13/(2d) and 9/d. The added             22

23   regression verifies these vertices rationally. The proof does not assert that these      23

24   convenient formulas remain valid for untested arbitrary d.                               24

25     The state covering radius of a nonuniform tensor grid is half the sum of its maxi- 25
26   mum coordinate spacings. Each action net is generated from a downward enclosure          26

27   of capacity. Its coverage is at most 1/(8K ) + 2−48 under the implemented binary64       27

28   construction. This last conservative allowance covers capacity enclosure, multiplica- 28
29   tion, division and the final downward displacement on the bounded action domain. 29
30   Fixed spacing and valid interval arithmetic are checked; a negative action is clipped    30

31   to zero. Downward quantization on deployment preserves feasibility because the           31

32   capacity itself is enclosed from below over the complete acquisition cell.               32
     64

1                    23. NUMERICAL LABELS AND THE SEPARATED ACCOUNT                                1

2
       An objective query has lower and upper endpoints for its midpoint-sum expec- 2
3                                                                                                  3
     tation, plus the deterministic continuous-law integration remainder. Labels are
4                                                                                                  4
     rounded on the inherited dyadic lattice, and the largest distance to the objective
5                                                                                                  5
     endpoints supplies the query allowance et . Selecting the least rounded label and
6                                                                                                  6
     retaining its original action costs one allowance in the label comparison and another
7                                                                                                  7
     in the complete selected-policy account. It does not require a second copy of the
8                                                                                                  8
     action-cover term in main equation (71).
9                                                                                                  9
       For a uniform scalar bin of width w, the mean absolute distance to its midpoint
10                                                                                                 10
     is w/4. Here w = 1/(16M) and the transition’s shock modulus is d. Therefore the
11                                                                                                 11
     numerical account decomposes into βdLt+1 /(64M) and the remaining arithmetic
12                                                                                                 12
     width. The recorded primitive coefficients in main equation (99) reconstruct each
13
     witness bound as a/N + b/K + q/M + η with a nonnegative numerical remainder. 13
14                                                                                                 14
     The release checks this decomposition at all witness rungs, as well as the full signed
15                                                                                                 15
     formula for every conventional rung.
16                                                                                                 16
       The continuous allocation in main Proposition 38 minimizes a leading product,
17                                                                                                 17
     not a full finite-precision program. With positive coefficient ci and exponent pi ,
18
     changing variables to error contribution xi = ci /ri gives the objective ∑i pi log xi . Its   18

19                                                                                                 19
     constrained first-order condition is xi = min( x̄i , pi λ). The multiplier exists uniquely
20                                                                                                 20
     until every cap binds. A zero coefficient has no benefit from refinement, so its
21                                                                                                 21
     coordinate stays at the resource lower bound. Upward dyadic rounding keeps every
22                                                                                                 22
     error term weakly smaller. Compiler setup, integer counts, coefficient bit lengths,
23                                                                                                 23
     adaptive pilots, memory and durable output are not removed by that relaxation.
24                                                                                                 24

                     24. WHY THE COMPILED ACTOR IS THE ORIGINAL ACTOR                              25

26     At a tensor node the distance transform minimizes over original sites, not merely           26

27   neighboring transformed nodes. Each forward relaxation along a coordinate line                27

28   compares the current value–owner pair with the preceding pair plus the weighted               28

29   coordinate distance; the backward pass does the same from the opposite direction. 29
30   The invariant is the lexicographic minimum over all processed original sites in the           30

31   processed coordinates. Separability proves the invariant after the final coordinate. 31
32   Keeping only the winning value would lose the actor identity.                                 32
                           NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                               65

1      For an off-grid point, choose a cell corner coordinatewise between the point and             1

2    its lexicographically first minimizing site. Distances add exactly. At that corner an          2

3    owner giving a smaller value, or a tied value and smaller original index, contradicts          3

4    the minimizer at the original point. This proves the owner identity even for incon- 4
5    sistent labels, zero slope, boundary cells and ties. Nonuniform coordinate spacings            5

6    enter only the exact distances.                                                                6

7      In interval execution, value expressions are enclosed outward. Actor candidates              7

8    whose lower objective exceeds another candidate’s upper objective can be discarded;            8

9    the remaining possibilities must be retained. An exact point tie is resolved by                9

10   rational comparison and the original index rule. A genuine uncertain state box is              10

11   not an exact point: the hull of its surviving actions is propagated. Boxes crossing            11

12   cells use the union of all relevant corners. These operations may cost more than one           12

13   point query, and the direct records retain actor and measurement ambiguity counts. 13
14                                                                                                  14

15                25. ACQUISITION FOR WITNESS AND CONVENTIONAL POLICIES                             15

16
       Let x ∈ C be the true state and xb the reported midpoint, with radius r in ℓ1 . Fix          16

17                                                                                                  17
     the selected original node xi and node action ai . The ideal repair at the true state
18
     is feasible and within K A ∥ x − xi ∥1 of ai . Replacing that repair by the cell-wide          18

19
     lower-capacity repair followed by downward spacing ∆ changes the action by at                  19

20
     most 2K A r + ∆. Thus                                                                          20

21                                                                                                  21

22                    Q( x, aimpl ) ≤ yi + e + Lgr ∥ x − xi ∥1 + D (2K A r + ∆).                    22

23                                                                                                  23

24   For a witness selection at xb, yi + Lgr ∥ xb − xi ∥1 = f ( xb), up to a separately certified   24

25   selector error. The triangle inequality and L f = Lgr yield the allowance in main              25

26   equation (73).                                                                                 26

27     For conventional nearest-node selection, subtract and add f ( xb). The expres- 27
28   sion yi + Lgr ∥ xb − xi ∥1 − f ( xb) is bounded by its complete nearest-cell maximum           28

29   Et . The remaining terms are at most ( Lgr + L f + 2DK A )r + D∆. This proves main             29

30   equation (109) without a continuity assumption on the actor. It also explains why              30

31   applying the witness coefficient unchanged to an interpolated conventional critic              31

32   would be incorrect when the actual critic modulus differs.                                     32
     66

1      For two-state records, r (b) = 2−b and the common spacing is 1/4096. The all-state    1

2    bit criterion is therefore G + K∆ + A2−b + 2λb ∑t<T βt , where A uses the appropriate   2

3    actual critic modulus and K∆ = ∑t βt Dt /4096. The interval-net criterion uses actual   3

                  26. CONTINUOUS PATH LAW AND SIMULTANEOUS INFERENCE
7                                                                                            7

8      Conditional on all construction records, the studied controllers are fixed. Each      8

9    simulation row draws independent uniform indices for the initial coordinates            9

10   and each innovation. The interval simulator encloses the complete continuous            10

11   cell corresponding to those indices; no bin midpoint is substituted for the true        11

12   path distribution in the estimand. Uniformly mixing conditional continuous cells        12

13   reproduces the original uniform initial and innovation laws. Common innovations         13

14   may couple two policies, but each follows its own endogenous trajectory.                14

15     In dimension two, a valid support interval for a raw discounted path-cost differ- 15
16   ence is [− H, H ], where                                                                16
                                      99 + 4p       37
17
                                 H=           ∑
                                         64 t<T
                                                βt + β T .
                                                    16
                                                                                             17

18                                                                                           18

19   Indeed, current cost is nonnegative and at most (99 + 4p)/64 by separately bound- 19
20   ing squared-target, imbalance, shortage and action costs. Terminal cost is at most      20

21   37/16. These conservative bounds apply to every feasible repaired path. Clipping        21

22   interval endpoints to the support preserves inclusion. Lower endpoint expectations      22

23   lie below the true cost difference; upper endpoint expectations lie above it.           23

24     The stored moments consist of outward sample-mean intervals and upper sample- 24
25   variance bounds for each endpoint sample. In (104), the square root is rounded          25

26   upward after checking its square against the rational radicand. The linear remain- 26
27   der is rational. With K ≤ 300, α = 1/100 and ℓ = 13, a finite positive Taylor sum       27

28   verifies e13 > 4K/α. The bounded empirical-Bernstein inequalities of Maurer and         28

29   Pontil (2009) and a union bound therefore yield the claimed simultaneous event. 29
30   Dependence between contrasts through shared economic objects is permitted; in- 30
31   dependence within each bin-index sample is the statistical assumption. The fixed        31

32   pseudorandom seeds do not prove that assumption.                                        32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                             67

1      A first-crossing choice is measurable with respect to construction information. 1
2    The conditional coverage assertion consequently remains valid after this choice. 2
3    A bit selector or a fee queried after seeing the simultaneous intervals also needs          3

4    no additional error allocation: on the one event, each underlying cost difference           4

5    already belongs to its interval. This does not validate new initial laws, new trained       5

6    policies, or unrecorded contrasts. Old and new study families have separate error           6

7    accounts; combining them incurs the corresponding union bound.                              7

10                      27. EXACT FRONTIERS AND DECISION SEMANTICS                               10

11                                                                                               11

12     For a fixed construction ladder, the first-crossing function can change only at a         12

13   recorded certificate. A sorted union of all such values, together with zero, partitions     13

14   all positive tolerances into left-closed, right-open intervals. At an attained endpoint     14

15   the weak inequality in the stopping rule applies. Later nonmonotone or dominated            15

16   certificates need not change the function, but remain in the raw record. Adjacent           16

17   intervals with identical returned rungs may be merged for display; the exact rational       17

18   partition is deposited.                                                                     18

19     For a direct interval [l, u], the gain from replacing witness by FVI is in [l, u];        19

20   the reverse gain is in [−u, −l ]. The fee max(0, u, −l ) suffices to certify that neither   20

21   switch is strictly profitable. The smaller max(0, l, −u) only says that neither switch      21

22   has an interval lying wholly in its strictly profitable region. When zero lies inside       22

23   a wide interval, this latter threshold is zero and is not evidence that a free switch       23

24   cannot pay. The release reports both objects.                                               24

25     For information price λ, each bit choice supplies lower and upper affine net-cost         25

26   lines. Enumerating all their nonnegative intersections partitions the price half-line. 26
27   On an open interval, the active lower and upper lines are fixed. At a boundary all          27

28   lines are compared exactly with smallest-bit ties. The difference between the active        28

29   upper and lower lines is the selected implementation’s valid regret upper bound. 29
30   The identity line at 16 bits is exactly zero before information charges. No simulation      30

31   count or time is assigned to an unperformed identity experiment; its arithmetic             31

32   identity is recorded explicitly.                                                            32

25     At fixed x, the transition is affine in a. The terminal objective is a sum of convex           25

26   quadratics and squared positive parts of affine functions. Its expectation is convex. 26
27   The action cost pa2 + 4a4 has second derivative 2p + 48a2 ≥ 2p. Thus final condi- 27
28   tional cost has a unique continuous minimizer, possibly on the capacity boundary. 28
29   Its lattice minimizer lies at the first lattice point with nonnegative derivative or the         29

30   preceding point, with boundary truncation. The implementation searches at most                   30

31   the fixed 4096 denominator’s range, compares the two costs, and uses exact rational              31

32   comparison when outward point intervals fail to decide a tie. The incumbent need                 32

                           30. SIMULTANEOUS ACTUAL-COST INFERENCE
16                                                                                                       16

17     The R50 primary family contains 25 pairs of baseline generators. Each group con- 17
18   tains four policies—the two incumbents and their final repairs—and produces four                    18

19   absolute costs and six pairwise differences. Thus there are 250 sampled estimands. 19
20   Their selection uses frozen construction information only. Within each group the                    20

21   continuous initial law and the first T − 1 innovations are represented by forty-bit                 21

22   bins with outward propagation. The last innovation is integrated analytically. Con- 22
23   ditional expectation over this last innovation preserves each policy’s expected total               23

24   cost. It reduces simulation variance without substituting a new innovation law.                     24

25     The path-cost support derived in the retained inference section applies in dimen- 25
26   sions two through four: the target and imbalance terms are coordinate averages                      26

27   with the same bounds, the shortage term is bounded by 1/2, and feasible actions                     27

28   do not exceed 1/4. Consequently 0 ≤ J-score ≤ H, with H as in Section 26; a paired                  28

29   score lies in [− H, H ]. An incumbent-minus-own-repair contrast is nonnegative by                   29

30   the independently proved gate and is bounded by H. Because the earlier trajec- 30
31   tory is common, its numerical enclosure uses the centered final contrast instead of                 31

32   subtracting two complete total-cost intervals.                                                      32
                          NEURAL BELLMAN OPERATORS: SUPPLEMENT                           71

1      For endpoint observations Xi ∈ [ A, B], the retained empirical-Bernstein bound          1

2    uses                                                                                      2
                                q
3                                                                                              3
                         Rn =       2Var( X )ℓ/n + 7( B − A)ℓ/[3(n − 1)],
4                                                                                              4

5    with outward moment intervals, an upper sample variance, and a rationally checked         5

6    upward square root. Lower endpoints and upper endpoints each receive a one- 6
7    sided bound. Set n = 131072, ℓ = 13 and α = 1/100. The family cap is 512; an exact        7

8    finite Taylor sum proves e13 > 4(512)/α. A union bound therefore covers all 250           8

9    actual-cost estimands under the independent-bin model. The cap leaves room but            9

10   does not certify unrecorded models or initial laws. The pseudorandom stream               10

11   and its hash establish reproducibility, not independence of ideal observations. The       11

12   confidence event for the retained R49 family is separate; combining old and new           12

13   statistical assertions incurs the sum of their failure probabilities.                     13

                          31. MECHANISM AND ECONOMIC FRONTIERS
15                                                                                             15

16     Denote witness and FVI by W, F and their final repairs by W + , F + . Pure algebra      16

17   yields                                                                                    17

18                                                                                             18
                      JW − JF = ( JW − JW + ) + ( JW + − JF+ ) − ( JF − JF+ ).           (4)
19                                                                                             19

20   All three terms concern actual costs. The first and last are direct measures of remov- 20
21   able final-action loss; the middle retains differences from earlier actions, acquired     21

22   states, continuation approximation and the gate’s conservatism. It is not identified      22

23   as a single causal contribution of neural representation. The experiment reports each     23

24   term, accepted and blocked cell counts, final action means, final state means and         24

25   earlier action means by date. This exposes how much of the original ordering can          25

26   be removed without retraining the continuation. It does not claim that the residual       26

27   has been exhaustively decomposed into mutually independent mechanisms.                    27

28     For finite intervals [ L j , Uj ], the upper-net-cost choice’s true regret is bounded   28

29   by the selected upper line minus the minimum lower line. To see why this is               29

30   conservative, consider two overlapping cost intervals: their midpoint ordering            30

31   may reverse within the same event. The upper-envelope decision remains valid but          31

32   cannot identify the true minimizer. Pairwise centered intervals can certify individual    32
     72

1    replacements more sharply than the absolute frontier. For any fixed installation fee,                                    1

2    transmission conversion factor and nonnegative resource price, add their declared                                        2

3    costs before applying the comparison. A new price does not require new sampling                                          3

4    on the same finite event. A new sensing policy does.                                                                     4
